If you have sites that support automatic background updates, the update process will begin automatically.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.1.5 is a short-cycle release. The next major release will be version 7.2, currently planned for December 8th.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">For more information, please visit the WordPress 7.1.5 <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/documentation/wordpress-version/version-7-1-5/\">HelpHub site</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Security updates included in this release</h2>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>A malformed regular expression that broadened JSON media type matching, fixed as a hardening measure, reported by Mo Jangda of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A multisite privilege escalation issue letting a site Administrator take over a Super Admin account via Customizer menu previews, reported by Yuval Miller.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A reflected XSS in the legacy media library popup, reported by Anthropic.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>An XSS in wp_interactivity_data_wp_context() via the store namespace, reported by <a href=\"https://phpc.social/@jrf_nl\">Juliette Reinders Folmer</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>SQL injection in WP_Meta_Query via nested relation values, reported by <a href=\"https://yougina.eu/\">Christiaan Swiers</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Unauthenticated information disclosure exposing private taxonomy terms via the REST API search endpoint, reported by Anthropic.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Author+ information disclosure exposing user email addresses via the REST API comments endpoint, reported by Ben Bidner of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>SQL injection via the front page setting in Customizer changeset previews, reported by Rudy Faile of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Contributor+ XSS via scripts in oEmbed provider responses, reported by vvh1te3zz.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Unauthenticated stored XSS via shortcode attributes in comments, reported by <a href=\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/rafiemuhammad/\">Rafie Muhammad</a> (Awesome Motive, Inc.).</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Unauthenticated denial of service via batched XML-RPC pingbacks, reported by <a href=\"https://novamira.ai\">Giovanni Cintolo</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Editor+ information disclosure via post and template author assignment in the REST API, reported by Jarda Snajdr of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Administrator stored XSS via Customizer previews, reported by Weston Ruter of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Author+ authorization bypass via comment editing in the admin, reported by Jesse McNeil.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Subscriber+ information disclosure via XML-RPC wp.getComments, reported by <a href=\"https://github.com/dlwodnr59\">Jaewook Lee (grze0)</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A hardening change that removes the block editor&#8217;s Imgur embed variation, whose server-side provider is already gone, reported by <a href=\"https://jackfromeast.github.io/\">Zhengyu Liu</a>, <a href=\"https://jesse-yang.com/\">Jingcheng Yang</a>, and <a href=\"https://gvzhong.github.io/\">Gavin Zhong</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Contributor+ stored XSS in the Comment Date block via its date format, reported by Weston Ruter and Jeremy Felt of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Unauthenticated privilege escalation via Template Part block attributes that could lead to remote code execution, reported by <a href=\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/logan-homolka/\">Logan Homolka</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>XSS in the block editor via Openverse image metadata, reported by <a href=\"https://kq5.jp/about/\">Shu Takahashi</a> of Velcore Consulting Inc.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Contributor+ stored XSS in the block editor via media filenames when converting a media block to a File block, reported by CryptoFox.</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Thank you to these WordPress contributors</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This release was led by <a class=\"wp-block-heading\">Backports</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">As a courtesy, the security fixes were backported to all branches eligible to receive security fixes (currently through 4.7). As a reminder, only the most recent version of WordPress is actively supported. The backports are in progress and will ship as they become ready.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">In WordPress 7.0, 1024 bit certificates were removed from bundled root certificates. This change has been applied to 6.9.12 and earlier as part of this release.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 RC 2 has also been released, containing all applicable WordPress 7.2 RC1 [&#8230;]\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:14:\"David Baumwald\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:40:\"http://purl.org/rss/1.0/modules/content/\";a:1:{s:7:\"encoded\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:10144:\"\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The first Release Candidate (“RC1”) for WordPress 7.2 is ready for download and testing!</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>This version of the WordPress software is still under development</strong>. Please do not install, run, or test this version of WordPress on production or mission-critical websites. Instead, it’s recommended to evaluate RC1 on a test server and site.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 RC1 can be tested using any of the following methods:</p>\n\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-table\"><table class=\"has-fixed-layout\"><tbody><tr><td><strong>Plugin</strong></td><td>Install and activate the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-beta-tester/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WordPress Beta Tester</a> plugin on a WordPress install. (Select the “Bleeding edge” channel and “Beta/RC Only” stream.)</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Direct Download</strong></td><td>Download the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/wordpress-7.2-RC1.zip\">RC1 version (zip)</a> and install it on a WordPress website.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Command Line</strong></td><td>Use this <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/cli/\">WP-CLI</a> command: <br><code>wp core update --version=7.2-RC1</code></td></tr><tr><td><strong>WordPress Playground</strong></td><td>Use the <a href=\"http://playground.wordpress.net/release\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WordPress Playground instance</a> to test the software directly in your browser.  No setup required – just click and go!</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The scheduled final release date for WordPress 7.2 is <strong>December 8, 2026</strong>. The full <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/2026/10/06/wordpress-7-2-release-party-schedule/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">release schedule can be found here</a>. Your help testing Beta and RC versions is vital to making this release as stable and powerful as possible. Thank you to everyone who contributes by testing!</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Please continue checking the <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Make WordPress Core blog</a> for <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/tag/7-2\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">7.2-related posts</a> in the coming weeks for more information.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Core Changes Since Beta 5</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Check out the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/news/2026/10/wordpress-7-2-beta-1-is-ready-for-download-and-testing/\">Beta 1 announcement</a> for details on WordPress 7.2.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can browse the technical details for all issues addressed since Beta 5 using these links:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/query?changetime=11%2F10%2F2026..11%2F17%2F2026&amp;milestone=7.2&amp;status=closed&amp;group=component&amp;order=id&amp;col=id&amp;col=milestone&amp;col=owner&amp;col=type&amp;col=priority\">Closed 7.2 WordPress Core Trac tickets</a> since November 10, 2026</li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/commits/wp/7.2?since=2026-11-10&amp;until=2026-11-17\">7.2 Gutenberg commits</a> since November 10, 2026</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 Release Candidate 1 brings additional bug fixes, accessibility improvements, and compatibility updates as the release moves closer to its December 8 launch.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Recent refinements include fixes to client-side media uploads, improvements to the new random-content redirect feature, and better keyboard navigation through a skip link in the WordPress admin bar. Additional updates improve plugin dependency messaging, script module translations, and the reliability of the HTML and Interactivity APIs.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Developers will also benefit from continued Abilities API refinements and updates to bundled libraries, including PHPMailer, Requests, getID3, and sodium_compat.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66490\">Trac #66490</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66681\">Trac #66681</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66625\">Trac #66625</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66331\">Trac #66331</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66591\">Trac #66591</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/65984\">Trac #65984</a></p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Additional Block Editor Improvements</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 RC1 includes further refinements to the block editor, addressing issues with duplicated block content, keyboard interactions on iOS, and revision previews. Improvements to the Interactivity API prevent outdated navigation responses from replacing newer content, while additional fixes improve plugin installation workflows, featured image compatibility, and link suggestions. The transition to modern SVG icons also continues throughout WordPress administration.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/86839\">GitHub #86839</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/86703\">GitHub #86703</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/86608\">GitHub #86608</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/78367\">GitHub #78367</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/86854\">GitHub #86854</a></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Want to know more about this release? Here are some highlights:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>New Ipsum default theme</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Expanded responsive design and Gallery controls</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Improved Global Styles and typography tools</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Enhanced Canvas Notes and collaboration</li>\n\n\n\n<li>New DataForm block settings inspector</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Faster media uploads and image processing</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Modernized WordPress admin with SVG icons</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Faster admin navigation and asset loading</li>\n\n\n\n<li>New “On This Day” widget and random post discovery</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Improved translation update management</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Accessibility, security, and PHP 8.6 compatibility improvements</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How you can contribute</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress is open source software made possible by a passionate community of people collaborating on and contributing to its development. The resources below outline various ways you can help the world’s most popular open source web platform, regardless of your technical expertise.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Get involved in testing</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Testing for issues is crucial to the development of any software. It’s also a meaningful way for anyone to contribute. </p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Your help testing the WordPress 7.2 RC1 version is key to ensuring that the final release is the best it can be. While testing the upgrade process is essential, trying out new features is equally important. This <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/2026/10/20/help-test-wordpress-7-2/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">detailed guide</a> will walk you through testing features in WordPress 7.2.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Update your theme or plugin</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">For plugin and theme authors, your products play an integral role in extending the functionality and value of WordPress for all users.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Thanks for continuing to test your themes and plugins with the WordPress 7.2 beta releases. With RC1, you’ll want to conclude your testing and update the <em>“Tested up to”</em> version in your <a href=\"https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/how-your-readme-txt-works/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">plugin’s readme file</a> to 7.2.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you find compatibility issues, please post detailed information to the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/support/forum/alphabeta/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">support forum</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Test on your hosting platforms</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Web hosts provide vital infrastructure for supporting WordPress and its users. Testing on hosting systems helps inform the development process while ensuring that WordPress and hosting platforms are fully compatible, free of errors, optimized for the best possible user experience, and that updates roll out to customer sites without issue.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Want to test WordPress on your hosting system? <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/hosting/test-results-getting-started/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Get started with configuring distributed hosting tests here</a>. Thank you to all <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/hosting/test-results/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">web hosts who help test WordPress</a>!</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Curious about testing releases in general? Follow along with the <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">testing initiatives in Make Core</a> and join the <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/messages/core-test/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">#core-test channel</a> on <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Making WordPress Slack</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">An RC1 haiku</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>The final bugs fall</em><br><em>Release candidate takes flight</em><br><em>Launch day draws nearer</em></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"has-text-align-right wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Props to <a href=\'https://profiles.wordpress.org/audrasjb/\' The plugin is available in the WordPress.org Plugin Directory, along with the command-line client used to copy the site. WordPress has long let you export your posts and pages. Moving the site itself means bringing along its theme, plugins, uploads, [&#8230;]\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:9:\"enclosure\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:0:\"\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:1:{s:0:\"\";a:3:{s:3:\"url\";s:52:\"https://videos.files.wordpress.com/KvhaIGjZ/pull.mp4\";s:6:\"length\";s:6:\"625628\";s:4:\"type\";s:9:\"video/mp4\";}}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:15:\"Adam Zieliński\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:40:\"http://purl.org/rss/1.0/modules/content/\";a:1:{s:7:\"encoded\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:5673:\"\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Reprint is a free, open-source, PHP-based tool that copies a WordPress site’s files and database. The plugin is available in the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/plugins/reprint/\">WordPress.org Plugin Directory</a>, along with the command-line client used to copy the site.</p>\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-post-featured-image\"><img data-recalc-dims=\"1\" fetchpriority=\"high\" decoding=\"async\" width=\"2560\" height=\"1440\" src=\"https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/11/reprint-hero-scaled.png?resize=2560%2C1440&#038;ssl=1\" class=\"attachment-post-thumbnail size-post-thumbnail wp-post-image\" alt=\"\" style=\"object-fit:cover;\" srcset=\"https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/11/reprint-hero-scaled.png?w=2560&amp;ssl=1 2560w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/11/reprint-hero-scaled.png?resize=300%2C169&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/11/reprint-hero-scaled.png?resize=1024%2C576&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/11/reprint-hero-scaled.png?resize=768%2C432&amp;ssl=1 768w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/11/reprint-hero-scaled.png?resize=1536%2C864&amp;ssl=1 1536w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/11/reprint-hero-scaled.png?resize=2048%2C1152&amp;ssl=1 2048w\" sizes=\"(max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px\" /></figure>\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress has long let you <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/documentation/article/tools-export-screen/\">export your posts and pages</a>. Moving the site itself means bringing along its theme, plugins, uploads, and settings, too. Making that easier has been part of <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/2024/06/21/data-liberation-and-wordpress-migrations/\">WordPress’s Data Liberation work</a> since 2024.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Example use cases</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can use Reprint to copy your site to a new host, or to create a local copy meant for testing.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">While moving your site to a new host, visitors can keep using the original. Use Reprint to copy the site, and then open the copy to check that your pages load, your images appear, and your forms work before you send visitors there. When you’re ready to switch, pause changes on the original site, make one last copy, and point your domain to the new host. The <a href=\"https://developer.wordpress.org/reprint/guides/move-to-another-host/\">step-by-step guide to moving your site</a> walks you through the move.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can also use Reprint to create a site copy meant for testing, a place to try changes without affecting the site your visitors use. <a href=\"https://developer.wordpress.org/reprint/getting-started/copy-a-site/\">Copy your site to your computer</a> or try the <a href=\"https://playground.wordpress.net/?clone-site=https%3A%2F%2Freprint-demo.wordpress.net\">experimental, browser-based option in WordPress Playground</a>. Use the copy to try a new layout with your existing pages, see how a plugin fits into your site, or check that everything still works after a WordPress update.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How Reprint copies a site</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Reprint sends your files and database directly to the computer or server you choose, one piece at a time, without needing to connect to an external service. The transfer can begin without your old host having to make room for an archive of the whole site.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If the connection drops during the copy, you can resume from the last saved point. If you give Reprint a new site address, it can update links that point to the old one. It builds on the <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/2025/11/27/wordpress-importer-can-now-migrate-urls-in-your-content/\">WordPress Importer’s approach to rewriting links</a> and applies it to the copied database, including links stored in site settings, blocks, shortcodes, and complex data structures like serialized data or custom fields safely without corrupting tables.</p>\n\n\n\n		<figure class=\"wp-block-jetpack-videopress jetpack-videopress-player\" style=\"\" >\n			<div class=\"jetpack-videopress-player__wrapper\"> <iframe title=\"VideoPress Video Player\" aria-label=\"VideoPress Video Player\" width=\"500\" height=\"293\" src=\"https://videopress.com/embed/KvhaIGjZ?cover=1&amp;autoPlay=1&amp;controls=0&amp;loop=1&amp;muted=1&amp;persistVolume=0&amp;playsinline=1&amp;preloadContent=metadata&amp;useAverageColor=1&amp;hd=0\" frameborder=\"0\" allowfullscreen data-resize-to-parent=\"true\" allow=\"clipboard-write; presentation\"></iframe><script src=\'https://v0.wordpress.com/js/next/videopress-iframe.js?m=1790791280\'></script></div>\n			\n			\n		</figure>\n		\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">When copying a site repeatedly, Reprint figures out what changed since the last time and copies only the files it needs.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Try Reprint</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You don’t need to connect your own site to see how Reprint works. Try the <a href=\"https://playground.wordpress.net/?clone-site=https%3A%2F%2Freprint-demo.wordpress.net\">experimental, browser-based option in WordPress Playground</a>. You can copy a sample site right in your browser, with nothing to install.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">When you’re ready to work with your own pages and settings, start with a test site you manage. The <a href=\"https://developer.wordpress.org/reprint/getting-started/copy-a-site/\">guide to making your first copy</a> takes you from installing the plugin to opening the copied site on your computer.</p>\n\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:30:\"com-wordpress:feed-additions:1\";a:1:{s:7:\"post-id\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:5:\"22001\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}}}i:3;a:6:{s:4:\"data\";s:60:\"\n		\n		\n		\n		\n		\n				\n		\n		\n\n					\n										\n					\n		\n		\n			\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";s:5:\"child\";a:4:{s:0:\"\";a:6:{s:5:\"title\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:38:\"WordCamp 11 Nov 2026 14:00:00 +0000\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:8:\"category\";a:2:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:9:\"Community\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}i:1;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:6:\"Events\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"guid\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:35:\"https://wordpress.org/news/?p=21919\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:1:{s:0:\"\";a:1:{s:11:\"isPermaLink\";s:5:\"false\";}}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:11:\"description\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:344:\"Next In 2026, WordCamp Asia [&#8230;]\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:17:\"Nicholas Garofalo\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:40:\"http://purl.org/rss/1.0/modules/content/\";a:1:{s:7:\"encoded\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:81524:\"\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Next year, WordCamp Asia 2027 heads to Penang, Malaysia. From April 9 to 11, the WordPress community will gather at the Penang Waterfront Convention Centre for three days of contribution, learning, and connection. Tickets are on sale and sponsorship applications are open, so now is the time to make your plans.</p>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-82d461e8 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex\">\n<div class=\"wp-block-button\"><a class=\"wp-block-button__link has-background wp-element-button\" href=\"https://asia.wordcamp.org/2027/tickets/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" style=\"background:linear-gradient(135deg,rgb(42,5,123) 0%,rgb(42,5,123) 100%)\" target=\"_blank\">Get Your Event Pass</a></div>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-button\"><a class=\"wp-block-button__link has-background wp-element-button\" href=\"https://asia.wordcamp.org/2027/call-for-sponsors/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" style=\"background:linear-gradient(135deg,rgb(42,5,123) 0%,rgb(42,5,123) 100%)\" target=\"_blank\">Become a Sponsor</a></div>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-button\"><a class=\"wp-block-button__link has-background wp-element-button\" href=\"https://asia.wordcamp.org/2027/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" style=\"background:linear-gradient(135deg,rgb(42,5,123) 0%,rgb(42,5,123) 100%)\" target=\"_blank\">About WordCamp Asia</a></div>\n</div>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">In 2026, <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/news/2026/04/celebrating-wcasia-2026/\">WordCamp Asia</a> brought 2,627 attendees to Mumbai, India. More than 1,500 participants joined Contributor Day, where table leads guided work on more than 20 contribution tables. The conference days that followed covered topics ranging from AI and developer workflows to enterprise WordPress and journalism on the open web, and closed with a panel featuring WordPress Executive Director <a href=\"https://profiles.wordpress.org/4thhubbard/\">Mary Hubbard</a>, <a href=\"https://profiles.wordpress.org/peterwilsoncc/\">Peter Wilson</a>, and <a href=\"https://profiles.wordpress.org/sergeybiryukov/\">Sergey Biryukov</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<blockquote class=\"wp-block-quote is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress is not a company. It is a shared commitment to keeping the web open.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Mary Hubbard, Executive Director, WordPress</em></p>\n</blockquote>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The mix of hands-on work and big-picture conversation is what sets a WordCamp apart. You learn from the people who build, scale, and maintain WordPress every day, trade ideas in the hallway between sessions, and head home with new collaborators and fresh energy for the work ahead.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Penang offers a memorable setting for WC Asia. George Town, a short drive from the venue, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, and National Geographic named Penang one of the best places in the world to travel to in 2027. The island is famous for its food, from internationally-recognized street food to fine dining, and it is one of Malaysia’s leading destinations for tourism. If you can, give yourself a day or two on either side of the conference to explore.</p>\n\n\n\n<figure data-wp-context=\"{&quot;galleryId&quot;:&quot;6afff5c538a4e&quot;}\" Contributor Day on April 9 has its own registration, which opens January 27, 2027. It is limited to 600 participants.</p>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-cover wp-elements-2\" In return, sponsors meet an engaged, international audience of developers, agencies, business owners, and creators.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Five packages are available, from a Small Business table to a Platinum booth, so there is a fit for local companies and global brands alike. How to Test WordPress 7.2 Beta [&#8230;]\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:14:\"David Baumwald\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:40:\"http://purl.org/rss/1.0/modules/content/\";a:1:{s:7:\"encoded\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:7814:\"\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 Beta 5 is ready for download and testing!</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>This beta release is intended for testing and development</strong> only. Please do not install, run, or test this version of WordPress on production or mission-critical websites. Instead, use a test environment or local site to explore the new features.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How to Test WordPress 7.2 Beta 5</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can test WordPress 7.2 Beta 5 in any of the following ways:</p>\n\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-table\"><table class=\"has-fixed-layout\"><tbody><tr><td><strong>WordPress Beta Tester Plugin</strong></td><td>Install and activate the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-beta-tester/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WordPress Beta Tester</a> plugin on a WordPress install. Select the “Bleeding edge” channel and “Beta/RC Only” stream.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Direct Download</strong></td><td>Download the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/wordpress-7.2-beta5.zip\">Beta 5 version (zip)</a> and install it on a WordPress website.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Command Line (WP-CLI)</strong></td><td>Use this <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/cli/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WP-CLI</a> command: <br><code>wp core update --version=7.2-beta5</code></td></tr><tr><td><strong>WordPress Playground</strong></td><td>Use a <a href=\"https://playground.wordpress.net/release\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">7.2 Beta 5 WordPress Playground instance</a> to test the software directly in your browser. No setup required-just click and go!</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The scheduled final release date for WordPress 7.2 is <strong><strong>December 8, 2026</strong></strong>. The full <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/2026/10/06/wordpress-7-2-release-party-schedule/\">release schedule can be found here</a>. Your help testing Beta and RC versions is vital to making this release as stable and powerful as possible. Thank you to everyone who contributes by testing!</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">What&#8217;s in WordPress 7.2 Beta 5?</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">For technical details on the issues addressed since Beta 4, see the following links:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/query?status=closed&amp;changetime=11%2F03%2F2026..11%2F10%2F2026&amp;milestone=7.2&amp;group=component&amp;col=id&amp;col=milestone&amp;col=owner&amp;col=type&amp;col=priority&amp;order=id\">Closed 7.2 WordPress Core Trac tickets</a> since November 3, 2026</li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/commits/wp/7.2?since=2026-11-03&amp;until=2026-11-10\">7.2 Gutenberg commits</a> since November 3, 2026</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Continued Improvements to WordPress 7.2</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 Beta 5 continues refining the upcoming release with additional security, accessibility, and compatibility improvements.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Recent changes include email notifications when application passwords are created, improved keyboard navigation in the WordPress admin toolbar, and accessibility refinements to Visual History. Additional fixes strengthen permissions for Notes reactions, improve compatibility with MP3 uploads, and address automatic update behavior in Git worktree installations.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/63927\">Trac #63927</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66331\">Trac #66331</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/65122\">Trac #65122</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66447\">Trac #66447</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/46544\">Trac #46544</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66460\">Trac #66460</a></p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Further Refinements to Ipsum</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The new default theme, Ipsum, receives another round of improvements in WordPress 7.2 Beta 5. Accessibility enhancements include improved keyboard focus indicators and view transitions that respect reduced-motion preferences. Additional refinements to block styles and patterns provide greater design flexibility, while optimized stylesheets and other under-the-hood improvements help prepare the theme for its final release.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66631\">Trac #66631</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/ipsum/pull/198\">GitHub #198</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/ipsum/pull/187\">GitHub #187</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/ipsum/pull/180\">GitHub #180</a></p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">PHP 8.6 Compatibility Improvements</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 aims to fully support PHP 8.6, which is scheduled to be released on November 19, 2026. The WordPress project has a long history of being compatible with new versions of PHP as soon as possible, and this release is no different.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">As of the Beta 5 release, all known compatibility issues with PHP 8.6 have been resolved.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">While all of the known issues in WordPress have been addressed, you will need to verify that all of your plugins and themes are also compatible with PHP 8.6 prior to upgrading. Keep an eye on the Making WordPress Core blog in the coming days for more detailed information about what to look for.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress continues to encourage all users to run the latest and greatest versions of PHP.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66648\">Trac #66648</a></p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How important is your testing?</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Testing for issues is a critical part of developing any software, and it’s a meaningful way for anyone to contribute – whether or not you have experience. <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/2026/10/20/help-test-wordpress-7-2/\">Details on what to test in WordPress 7.2 are available here</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you encounter an issue, please share it in the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/support/forum/alphabeta/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Alpha/Beta area</a> of the support forums. If you are comfortable submitting a reproducible bug report, you can do so <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/newticket\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">via WordPress Trac</a>. You can also check your issue against this list of <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/tickets/major\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">known bugs</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Curious about testing releases in general and how to get started? Follow along with the <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/\">testing initiatives in Make Core</a> and join the <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/messages/core-test/\">#core-test channel</a> on <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/\">Making WordPress Slack</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">A Beta 5 haiku</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>One last beta blooms<br>Release candidate awaits<br>The finish draws near</em></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"has-text-align-right wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Props to <a href=\'https://profiles.wordpress.org/audrasjb/\' +0000\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:8:\"category\";a:3:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:9:\"Education\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}i:1;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:6:\"Events\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}i:2;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:10:\"wp-credits\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"guid\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:35:\"https://wordpress.org/news/?p=21881\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:1:{s:0:\"\";a:1:{s:11:\"isPermaLink\";s:5:\"false\";}}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:11:\"description\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:380:\"More The program launched with the University [&#8230;]\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:12:\"Isotta Peira\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:40:\"http://purl.org/rss/1.0/modules/content/\";a:1:{s:7:\"encoded\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:4917:\"\n<figure class=\"wp-block-image size-large\"><img data-recalc-dims=\"1\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\" width=\"1024\" height=\"576\" alt=\"\" class=\"wp-image-21882\" src=\"https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Upside-down-lightbulb-sketches-in-notebook.png?resize=1024%2C576&#038;ssl=1\" srcset=\"https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Upside-down-lightbulb-sketches-in-notebook.png?resize=1024%2C576&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Upside-down-lightbulb-sketches-in-notebook.png?resize=300%2C169&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Upside-down-lightbulb-sketches-in-notebook.png?resize=767%2C431&amp;ssl=1 767w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Upside-down-lightbulb-sketches-in-notebook.png?resize=1536%2C864&amp;ssl=1 1536w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Upside-down-lightbulb-sketches-in-notebook.png?w=1672&amp;ssl=1 1672w\" sizes=\"auto, (max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px\" /></figure>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">More than 50 educational institutions have joined <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/education/credits/\">WordPress Credits</a>, giving students worldwide a way to earn academic recognition for contributing to WordPress. The program had two participating institutions in September 2025 and four the following month. By June 2026, that number had reached 27, before passing 50 this October.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The program launched with the University of Pisa and was announced at WordCamp Europe 2025. Since then, it has expanded through partnerships that connect students with experienced contributors and practical projects. The <a href=\"https://wordpress.github.io/WPCredits/\">WordPress Credits dashboard</a> recorded participating institutions across 17 countries as of October 19, including Bangladesh, Costa Rica, Spain, Bolivia, and Indonesia. Each institution can adapt the program to its academic requirements, allowing students to contribute as part of their studies.</p>\n\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-image size-large\"><img data-recalc-dims=\"1\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\" width=\"1024\" height=\"431\" alt=\"\" class=\"wp-image-21883\" src=\"https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Screenshot-2026-10-23-at-1.16.32-pm.png?resize=1024%2C431&#038;ssl=1\" srcset=\"https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Screenshot-2026-10-23-at-1.16.32-pm-scaled.png?resize=1024%2C431&amp;ssl=1 1024w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Screenshot-2026-10-23-at-1.16.32-pm-scaled.png?resize=300%2C126&amp;ssl=1 300w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Screenshot-2026-10-23-at-1.16.32-pm-scaled.png?resize=767%2C323&amp;ssl=1 767w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Screenshot-2026-10-23-at-1.16.32-pm-scaled.png?resize=1536%2C647&amp;ssl=1 1536w, https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/10/Screenshot-2026-10-23-at-1.16.32-pm-scaled.png?resize=2048%2C862&amp;ssl=1 2048w\" sizes=\"auto, (max-width: 1000px) 100vw, 1000px\" /></figure>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Learn through contribution</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Students work with mentors on projects that reflect their interests and areas of study. They might translate WordPress, improve documentation, create learning materials, or contribute to development and testing. Alongside that work, they practice collaborating with others and responding to feedback, then present their contributions at the end of the program. By October 19, the dashboard recorded 364 graduates and student contributions across 18 WordPress teams. That work supports the software and resources people use throughout the WordPress community.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Help the program grow</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Local contributors have helped establish these partnerships by introducing WordPress Credits to nearby schools and adapting it to local languages and educational settings. Mentors guide students through their first contributions, while sponsors provide support and resources. Educators help make that participation part of students’ academic work. Thank you to the students, educators, mentors, and supporters whose involvement has brought the program to more than 50 institutions.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Institutions can include WordPress Credits in an existing course, offer it as an internship, or incorporate it into a final project. The program is available at no cost to partner institutions, with flexible formats to suit their students and curriculum. To get started, read <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/community/handbook/education/credits/how-to-participate/institutions/\">how to launch a WordPress Credits program</a> and <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/education/credits/\">register your institution’s interest through the program page</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<p Wait, what happened to 7.2 Beta [&#8230;]\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:14:\"David Baumwald\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:40:\"http://purl.org/rss/1.0/modules/content/\";a:1:{s:7:\"encoded\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:7745:\"\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 Beta 4 is ready for download and testing!</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>This beta release is intended for testing and development</strong> only. Please do not install, run, or test this version of WordPress on production or mission-critical websites. Instead, use a test environment or local site to explore the new features.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Wait, what happened to 7.2 Beta 3???</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https://wordpress.org/news/2026/10/wordpress-7-1-4/\">WordPress 7.1.4 was released on October 28</a> which included WordPress 7.2 Beta 3.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How to Test WordPress 7.2 Beta 4</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can test WordPress 7.2 Beta 4 in any of the following ways:</p>\n\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-table\"><table class=\"has-fixed-layout\"><tbody><tr><td><strong>WordPress Beta Tester Plugin</strong></td><td>Install and activate the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-beta-tester/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WordPress Beta Tester</a> plugin on a WordPress install. Select the “Bleeding edge” channel and “Beta/RC Only” stream.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Direct Download</strong></td><td>Download the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/wordpress-7.2-beta4.zip\">Beta 4 version (zip)</a> and install it on a WordPress website.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Command Line (WP-CLI)</strong></td><td>Use this <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/cli/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WP-CLI</a> command: <br><code>wp core update --version=7.2-beta4</code></td></tr><tr><td><strong>WordPress Playground</strong></td><td>Use a <a href=\"https://playground.wordpress.net/release\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">7.2 Beta 4 WordPress Playground instance</a> to test the software directly in your browser. No setup required-just click and go!</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The scheduled final release date for WordPress 7.2 is <strong><strong>December 8, 2026</strong></strong>. The full <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/2026/10/06/wordpress-7-2-release-party-schedule/\">release schedule can be found here</a>. Your help testing Beta and RC versions is vital to making this release as stable and powerful as possible. Thank you to everyone who contributes by testing!</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">What&#8217;s in WordPress 7.2 Beta 4?</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">For technical details on the issues addressed since Beta 2, see the following links:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/query?status=closed&amp;changetime=10%2F27%2F2026..11%2F03%2F2026&amp;milestone=7.2&amp;group=component&amp;col=id&amp;col=milestone&amp;col=owner&amp;col=type&amp;col=priority&amp;order=id\">Closed 7.2 WordPress Core Trac tickets</a> since October 27, 2026</li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/commits/wp/7.2?since=2026-10-27&amp;until=2026-11-03\">7.2 Gutenberg commits</a> since October 27, 2026</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Continued Improvements to WordPress 7.2</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 Beta 4 continues refining the upcoming release with improvements to accessibility, editor reliability, and developer APIs, alongside additional bug fixes and compatibility updates.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Recent work includes improved keyboard focus visibility in Windows High Contrast mode, refinements to administration interfaces, and fixes for right-to-left block alignment and custom block spacing.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Developers will also benefit from continued improvements to the Abilities API, DataViews extensibility, and REST API behavior. Additional fixes strengthen revision handling, input validation, and compatibility across supported environments.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/45910\">Trac #45910</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66382\">Trac #66382</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66427\">Trac #66427</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66383\">Trac #66383</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66343\">Trac #66343</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66329\">Trac #66329</a></p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Continued Improvements to Ipsum</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The new default theme, Ipsum, continues to receive refinements ahead of WordPress 7.2&#8217;s final release. Recent updates improve accessibility with clearer keyboard focus indicators, more descriptive screen reader links, and better support for right-to-left languages. Additional improvements refine block patterns, touch target spacing, and color palette compatibility with other default themes.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66352\">Trac #66352</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/wordpress-develop/pull/15113\">GitHub #15113</a></p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Improved Translation Update Management</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 makes managing translation updates easier by displaying individual available updates on the Updates screen. Administrators can now review and selectively install translation updates, bringing language management in line with the familiar plugin and theme update experience.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/42281\">Trac #42281</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/wordpress-develop/pull/11610\">GitHub #11610</a></p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How important is your testing?</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Testing for issues is a critical part of developing any software, and it’s a meaningful way for anyone to contribute – whether or not you have experience. <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/2026/10/20/help-test-wordpress-7-2/\">Details on what to test in WordPress 7.2 are available here</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you encounter an issue, please share it in the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/support/forum/alphabeta/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Alpha/Beta area</a> of the support forums. If you are comfortable submitting a reproducible bug report, you can do so <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/newticket\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">via WordPress Trac</a>. You can also check your issue against this list of <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/tickets/major\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">known bugs</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Curious about testing releases in general and how to get started? Follow along with the <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/\">testing initiatives in Make Core</a> and join the <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/messages/core-test/\">#core-test channel</a> on <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/\">Making WordPress Slack</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">A Beta 4 haiku</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Where did Beta Three If you have sites that support automatic background updates, the update process will begin automatically.<br>WordPress 7.1.4 is a short-cycle release. The next major release will be <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/7-2/\">version 7.2</a>, currently planned for December 8th.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">For more information, in this release</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The security team would like to thank the following people and organizations for responsibly reporting vulnerabilities, and allowing them to be fixed in this release:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>A weakness in the WordPress upgrade process which can lead to remote code execution during the upgrade window, reported by <a href=\"https://www.picussecurity.com/resource/author/umut-bayram\">Umut Bayram</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A stored XSS with specially crafted block markup, reported by <a href=\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/rafiemuhammad/\">Rafie Muhammad</a> (Awesome Motive, Inc.).</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Privacy Data Removal Requests can be created in a way that leads to stored XSS, reported by Anthropic.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Multiple stored XSS issues in the Interactivity API independently discovered by AnC and DatNLQ from VietSunshine Cyber Security, rekter0 and Anas cherni, <a href=\"https://decentsecurity.io/\">Marcel Karlsson (whoismarcel)</a>, and Weston Ruter, Jon Surrell, Alex Concha, and Luis Herranz of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A stored denial of service issue in the Interactivity API, reported by Jon Surrell of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A stored XSS in Customizer selective refresh, reported by <a href=\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/rafiemuhammad/\">Rafie Muhammad</a> (Awesome Motive, Inc.).</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A Stored XSS in the Categories block reported by Ben Bidner of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Specially crafted User Logins can lead to a stored XSS reported by Anthropic.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Unpublished customizer changesets can be discovered by unauthenticated users, reported by <a href=\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/antoniolorensius/\">Antonio Lorensius Silaban</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A Stored XSS in Playlist and image blocks, reported by Ben Bidner of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Stored XSS in <code>core/comments-pagination-next</code> and <code>core/comments-pagination-previous</code> blocks, reported by Alex Concha of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Notes can be read by privileged users that lack access to edit the post the note is attached to, reported by Ramon of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>An update to the bundled root certificates due to the eMudhra revocation.</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Thank you to these WordPress contributors</h2>\n\n\n\n<p Please do not install, run, or test this version of WordPress on production or mission-critical websites. Instead, use a test environment or local site to explore the new features.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How to Test WordPress 7.2 Beta 2</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can test WordPress 7.2 Beta 2 in any of the following ways:</p>\n\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-table\"><table class=\"has-fixed-layout\"><tbody><tr><td><strong>WordPress Beta Tester Plugin</strong></td><td>Install and activate the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-beta-tester/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WordPress Beta Tester</a> plugin on a WordPress install. Select the “Bleeding edge” channel and “Beta/RC Only” stream.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Direct Download</strong></td><td>Download the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/wordpress-7.2-beta2.zip\">Beta 2 version (zip)</a> and install it on a WordPress website.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Command Line (WP-CLI)</strong></td><td>Use this <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/cli/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WP-CLI</a> command: <br><code>wp core update --version=7.2-beta2</code></td></tr><tr><td><strong>WordPress Playground</strong></td><td>Use a <a href=\"https://playground.wordpress.net/?site-slug=kind-classic-mountain&amp;networking=no&amp;php=8.3&amp;wp=beta&amp;language=&amp;multisite=no\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">7.2 Beta 2 WordPress Playground instance</a> to test the software directly in your browser. No setup required-just click and go!</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The scheduled final release date for WordPress 7.2 is <strong><strong>December 8, 2026</strong></strong>. The full <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/2026/10/06/wordpress-7-2-release-party-schedule/\">release schedule can be found here</a>. Your help testing Beta and RC versions is vital to making this release as stable and powerful as possible. Thank you to everyone who contributes by testing!</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">What&#8217;s in WordPress 7.2 Beta 2?</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">For technical details on the issues addressed since Beta 1, see the following links:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/query?status=closed&amp;changetime=10%2F21%2F2026..10%2F28%2F2026&amp;milestone=7.2&amp;group=component&amp;col=id&amp;col=milestone&amp;col=owner&amp;col=type&amp;col=priority&amp;order=id\">Closed 7.2 WordPress Core Trac tickets</a> since October 20, 2026</li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/commits/wp/7.2?since=2026-10-21&amp;until=2026-10-28\">7.2 Gutenberg commits</a> since October 20, 2026</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Continued Improvements to WordPress 7.2</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 Beta 2 builds on the first beta with additional enhancements, bug fixes, and refinements across the administration experience and developer APIs.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Development continues on the <strong>Abilities API</strong>, including content querying, capability requirements, and improvements to schema handling and permissions. New server-side registration capabilities for <strong>DataViews and DataForm</strong> also provide developers with more ways to extend WordPress interfaces.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Additional work includes improvements to REST API flexibility, admin asset loading, and the transition to modern SVG icons throughout WordPress administration.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66268\">Trac #66268</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66325\">Trac #66325</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66229\">Trac #66229</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66343\">Trac #66343</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/57548\">Trac #57548</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/65089\">Trac #65089</a></p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How important is your testing?</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Testing for issues is a critical part of developing any software, and it’s a meaningful way for anyone to contribute – whether or not you have experience. <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/2026/10/20/help-test-wordpress-7-2/\">Details on what to test in WordPress 7.2 are available here</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you encounter an issue, please share it in the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/support/forum/alphabeta/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Alpha/Beta area</a> of the support forums. If you are comfortable submitting a reproducible bug report, you can do so <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/newticket\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">via WordPress Trac</a>. You can also check your issue against this list of <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/tickets/major\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">known bugs</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Curious about testing releases in general and how to get started? Follow along with the <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/\">testing initiatives in Make Core</a> and join the <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/messages/core-test/\">#core-test channel</a> on <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/\">Making WordPress Slack</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">A Beta 2 haiku</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Second beta takes shape<br>New abilities take root<br>WordPress grows stronger</em></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"has-text-align-right wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Props to <a href=\'https://profiles.wordpress.org/mamaduka/\' Your web host may already be working to update PHP and put web application firewall (WAF) rules in place to mitigate the vulnerabilities, but you should still check for updates from your web host and, if necessary, update PHP as soon as possible.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Within WordPress, you can see which version of PHP your site runs under Tools &gt; Site Health &gt; Info &gt; Server. Many web hosts provide this information in your web hosting administration panel too.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">These vulnerabilities are <em>not</em> specific to WordPress and affect any server that hosts a PHP application. No WordPress update is required or planned for these issues.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Versions of PHP prior to 8.2 no longer receive security updates from the PHP project.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If your site runs a version prior to this and you manage your own web hosting, you should <em>consider taking your server offline</em> while you prepare to update to a newer version of PHP. This advice is being published by several national cyber security agencies. To quote <a href=\"https://www.ncsc.gov.uk/news/critical-security-issue-affecting-php\">the UK National Cyber Security Centre</a>:</p>\n\n\n\n<blockquote class=\"wp-block-quote is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Where immediate remediation is not feasible, organisations should consider taking the system offline until it can be fully mitigated.</p>\n</blockquote>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Further information on wordpress.org:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><a href=\"https://wordpress.org/support/update-php/\">Get a faster, more secure website: update PHP today</a></li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/handbook/references/php-compatibility-and-wordpress-versions/\">PHP Compatibility and WordPress Versions</a></li>\n\n\n\n<li><a class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This WordPress plugin, <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/plugins/photo-competition-manager/\">Photo Competition Manager</a>, is for photography clubs that want to run photography competitions. It allows members to upload photos and then vote on each, displaying them in a slideshow if the images are to be projected. Voting is done through a mobile interface. Results are shown immediately.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This version is a significant upgrade but many of the changes are not visible to the end user. The backend was ripped apart and improved so it runs better. Various bugs were squashed, but two major features were added as well:</p>\n\n\n\n<ol class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>External judges can judge the photos rather than asking the members to vote. A panel of judges is created and can be individually added to a competition. They are emailed (or given) a link to judge and use a similar interface to the mobile voting interface used previously.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>I added support for interclub competitions which adds more conditions to the competition. It&#8217;s currently based on the <a href=\"https://sacc.ie/\">SACC</a> interclub competition rules (X+2 substitute images, maximum Y images per photographer) but I expect we&#8217;ll add to that in the future. </li>\n</ol>\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-image\">\n<figure class=\"aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container\"><img alt=\"\" class=\"wp-image-89593250\" height=\"1640\" fill=\"#fff\">\n			</svg>\n		</button></figure>\n</div>\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-image\">\n<figure class=\"aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container\"><img alt=\"\" class=\"wp-image-89593253\" .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z\" fill=\"#fff\">\n			</svg>\n		</button></figure>\n</div>\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-image\">\n<figure class=\"aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container\"><img alt=\"\" class=\"wp-image-89593255\" height=\"2334\" src=\"https://odd.blog/files/2026/11/upload-1.avif\" width=\"1836\" /><button class=\"lightbox-trigger\" type=\"button\">\n			<svg fill=\"none\" height=\"12\" viewBox=\"0 0 12 12\" width=\"12\" xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\">\n				<path d=\"M2 0a2 2 0 0 0-2 2v2h1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 1 .5-.5h2V0H2Zm2 10.5H2a.5.5 0 0 1-.5-.5V8H0v2a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h2v-1.5ZM8 12v-1.5h2a.5.5 0 0 0 .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z\" fill=\"#fff\">\n			</svg>\n		</button></figure>\n</div>\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-image\">\n<figure class=\"aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container\"><img alt=\"\" class=\"wp-image-89593257\" height=\"1360\" src=\"https://odd.blog/files/2026/11/voting-controls-1.avif\" width=\"2560\" /><button class=\"lightbox-trigger\" type=\"button\">\n			<svg fill=\"none\" height=\"12\" viewBox=\"0 0 12 12\" width=\"12\" xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\">\n				<path d=\"M2 0a2 2 0 0 0-2 2v2h1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 1 .5-.5h2V0H2Zm2 10.5H2a.5.5 0 0 1-.5-.5V8H0v2a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h2v-1.5ZM8 12v-1.5h2a.5.5 0 0 0 .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z\" fill=\"#fff\">\n			</svg>\n		</button></figure>\n</div>\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">A typical workflow would consist of:</p>\n\n\n\n<ol class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Decide if you&#8217;re going to ask your members to vote or use external judges. If it&#8217;s judges, then set them up with their email addresses on the Judges page.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Set up the required pages on the Setup Wizard page. Those are voting and results pages.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Create a competition. I would set the start date to 1 or 2 weeks before the judging night, and set the close date to the day after judging night.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>You will already have added the members of your club through the Members page, so with uploads open, send them the upload link.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Hopefully uploaded images will start appearing over the next few days.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>On judging night, the Voting Controls page is used to close uploads, display the slideshow (optional if it&#8217;s a print competition) and open voting.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>The Voting Controls page will show a QR code for the voting page if you&#8217;re using the password system. Voting by members this way is an honour system. They enter their name, the password and then rate each photo as it is displayed on the slideshow. There is also a voting link system where a link is sent to each member.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Voting is closed. If desired, a critique slideshow can be done next, and then we move on to the next category.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>When the competition is done, click on the &#8220;Show Results&#8221; button and then the Results page to display the results to the members.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>On the dashboard Results page, there&#8217;s a button to email the members their individual scores. A CSV of the results is also available on this page.</li>\n</ol>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I&#8217;ve run several test competitions using the development site I use <a href=\"https://github.com/donnchawp/photo-competition-manager\">here</a>, but I welcome bug reports. The 0.3.0 version was used by Blarney Photography Club for the last year and worked with minimum problems, but I spent a lot of time on this version in the last 2 months so the more eyes on it the better.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you don&#8217;t have a WordPress site, I can recommend WordPress.com. The <a href=\"https://wordpress.com/pricing/\">personal plan</a> there is currently €4/month and lets you run plugins. I work for Automattic, who run WordPress.com, so of course I&#8217;d recommend them. <img alt=\"🙂\" class=\"wp-smiley\" src=\"https://s.w.org/images/core/emoji/17.0.2/72x72/1f642.png\" style=\"height: 1em;\" /></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"></p>\n\n#PhotoCompetitionManager #Photography #WordPress 22:39:58 Release\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"guid\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:35:\"https://wordpress.org/news/?p=22035\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"link\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:59:\"https://wordpress.org/news/2026/11/wordpress-7-1-5-release/\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:11:\"description\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:7325:\"<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This security release features fixes for multiple issues.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Because this is a security release, it is recommended that you update your sites immediately.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/download/\">download WordPress 7.1.5 from WordPress.org</a>, or visit your WordPress Dashboard, click “Updates”, and then click “Update Now”. If you have sites that support automatic background updates, the update process will begin automatically.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.1.5 is a short-cycle release. The next major release will be version 7.2, currently planned for December 8th.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">For more information, please visit the WordPress 7.1.5 <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/documentation/wordpress-version/version-7-1-5/\">HelpHub site</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Security updates included in this release</h2>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>A malformed regular expression that broadened JSON media type matching, fixed as a hardening measure, reported by Mo Jangda of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A multisite privilege escalation issue letting a site Administrator take over a Super Admin account via Customizer menu previews, reported by Yuval Miller.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A reflected XSS in the legacy media library popup, reported by Anthropic.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>An XSS in wp_interactivity_data_wp_context() via the store namespace, reported by <a href=\"https://phpc.social/@jrf_nl\">Juliette Reinders Folmer</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>SQL injection in WP_Meta_Query via nested relation values, reported by <a href=\"https://yougina.eu/\">Christiaan Swiers</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Unauthenticated information disclosure exposing private taxonomy terms via the REST API search endpoint, reported by Anthropic.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Author+ information disclosure exposing user email addresses via the REST API comments endpoint, reported by Ben Bidner of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>SQL injection via the front page setting in Customizer changeset previews, reported by Rudy Faile of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Contributor+ XSS via scripts in oEmbed provider responses, reported by vvh1te3zz.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Unauthenticated stored XSS via shortcode attributes in comments, reported by <a href=\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/rafiemuhammad/\">Rafie Muhammad</a> (Awesome Motive, Inc.).</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Unauthenticated denial of service via batched XML-RPC pingbacks, reported by <a href=\"https://novamira.ai\">Giovanni Cintolo</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Editor+ information disclosure via post and template author assignment in the REST API, reported by Jarda Snajdr of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Administrator stored XSS via Customizer previews, reported by Weston Ruter of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Author+ authorization bypass via comment editing in the admin, reported by Jesse McNeil.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Subscriber+ information disclosure via XML-RPC wp.getComments, reported by <a href=\"https://github.com/dlwodnr59\">Jaewook Lee (grze0)</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>A hardening change that removes the block editor&#8217;s Imgur embed variation, whose server-side provider is already gone, reported by <a href=\"https://jackfromeast.github.io/\">Zhengyu Liu</a>, <a href=\"https://jesse-yang.com/\">Jingcheng Yang</a>, and <a href=\"https://gvzhong.github.io/\">Gavin Zhong</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Contributor+ stored XSS in the Comment Date block via its date format, reported by Weston Ruter and Jeremy Felt of the WordPress Security Team.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Unauthenticated privilege escalation via Template Part block attributes that could lead to remote code execution, reported by <a href=\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/logan-homolka/\">Logan Homolka</a>.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>XSS in the block editor via Openverse image metadata, reported by <a href=\"https://kq5.jp/about/\">Shu Takahashi</a> of Velcore Consulting Inc.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Contributor+ stored XSS in the block editor via media filenames when converting a media block to a File block, reported by CryptoFox.</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Thank you to these WordPress contributors</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This release was led by <a class=\"wp-block-heading\">Backports</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">As a courtesy, the security fixes were backported to all branches eligible to receive security fixes (currently through 4.7). As a reminder, only the most recent version of WordPress is actively supported. The backports are in progress and will ship as they become ready.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">In WordPress 7.0, 1024 bit certificates were removed from bundled root certificates. This change has been applied to 6.9.12 and earlier as part of this release.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 RC 2 has also been released, containing all applicable fixes.</p>\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:7:\"pubDate\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:31:\"Wed, 18 Nov 2026 17:08:59 +0000\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:11:\"Jeremy Felt\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}}}i:3;a:6:{s:4:\"data\";s:21:\"\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";s:5:\"child\";a:2:{s:0:\"\";a:5:{s:5:\"title\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:84:\"Open Channels FM: The Real Challenge of AI in Open Source, Maintaining What We Build\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"guid\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:34:\"https://openchannels.fm/?p=2565627\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"link\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:90:\"https://openchannels.fm/the-real-challenge-of-ai-in-open-source-maintaining-what-we-build/\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:11:\"description\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:271:\"AI significantly accelerates code writing and participation in open source, improving contributor productivity by over 50%.
