class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:00:19] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> Welcome to the Jukebox Podcast from WP Tavern. My name is Nathan Wrigley.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Jukebox is a podcast which is dedicated to all things WordPress. The people, the events, the plugins, the blocks, the themes, and in this case, how WordPress adapts to rapid change the .com bubble to AI hysteria.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you&#8217;d like to subscribe to the podcast, you can do that by searching for WP Tavern in your podcast player of choice, or by going to wptavern.com/feed/podcast, and you can copy that URL into most podcast players.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you have a topic that you&#8217;d like us to feature on the podcast, I&#8217;m keen to hear from you and hopefully get you, or your idea, featured on the show. Head to wptavern.com/contact/jukebox, and use the form there.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So on the podcast today we have Mark Westguard. Mark has been building websites for over 30 years, starting out in the early days of the web with his agency, Absolute Internet in the UK, before moving to the US and transitioning into product development. He now runs WS Form, a form plugin for WordPress.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">During this episode, we get into the evolution of the internet and WordPress through major technological inflexion points. Most recently, the rise of AI. Mark draws comparisons between the AI era and the frenzy of the .com boom, sharing how rapid influxes of investments and hype, can reshape the entire landscape, often faster than the underlying technology can deliver sustainable results.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The conversation focused not just on the opportunities AI brings to developers and end users, such as dramatically lowering the barrier to entry for building websites or plugins, but also the risks and challenges.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">We chat about the overwhelming pace of new product creation, and how the real difficulty today isn&#8217;t necessarily building a plugin, but successfully marketing, supporting and sustaining it in a crowded ecosystem.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Towards the end, we talk about the potential saturation of plugin marketplaces and what this means for the future of WordPress businesses, especially as AI continues to accelerate product development.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Mark says that he&#8217;s embracing AI in his own workflow, but has some nostalgia for the days when coding was a hands-on craft.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Several other points were raised, including the ongoing demand for trusted, supported, software among non-developers, the looming spectre of AI driven security vulnerabilities, and whether traditional CMS platforms like WordPress might see a renaissance if AI hype collapses.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you&#8217;re curious about how AI is shaping, and challenging, the future of WordPress development and product businesses, this episode is for you.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you&#8217;re interested in finding out more, you can find all of the links in the show notes by heading to wptavern.com/podcast, where you&#8217;ll find all the other episodes as well.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">And so without further delay, I bring you Mark Westguard.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I am joined on the podcast by Mark Westguard. Hello, Mark.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:03:34] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> Hello Nathan, how are you?</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:03:35] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> Yeah, good. We&#8217;re going to have a little bit of a chat today about the landscape of WordPress in the era of AI. Let&#8217;s explore and see where we go.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So Mark, before we begin, bio time, basically, would you give us your little bio? Tell us what you do in the WordPress space.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:03:50] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> In the WordPress space, currently I&#8217;m running, WS Form, which is an online form plugin for WordPress.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">And I&#8217;ve been in the web game far too long. People can&#8217;t see our grey hairs, but I&#8217;ve been building websites for, oh, I want to say about 30 odd years now.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Yeah, I&#8217;ve gone through everything from Netscape Navigator through to where we are now with AI. So, have been building websites for many many companies, for many many years.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So I had a agency in the UK called Absolute Internet back in the day, back in 1996. And then moved to the States in 2005 and carried on with that, and then got into product about, I want to say eight years ago now. And have been enjoying it, but still dabbling in the agency side of things as well.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:04:34] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> Okay, so you&#8217;ve had a long and storied history. Has there ever been a moment where it&#8217;s felt like so much was in flux, in your past? Because, I think anybody really listening to this would have some intuition that AI is a moment of great inflexion point. Maybe this is par for the course though, over a 30 year period in the internet. Has there been anything which has disrupted things or changed things as rapidly as this?</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:04:56] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> I think, for me it was strange because the internet was there when I started university, but definitely was a big game changer for how we accessed information and searched for information.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The next big thing for me was really the .com bubble, and that going bang quite tremendously, which pulled my first agency into the ground. I mean, we went from 40 odd staff to four people in a day. So that was certainly a big flux in the internet industry that hit me.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Once that recovered, it&#8217;s been fairly plain sailing, hasn&#8217;t it? There&#8217;s been a lot of gentle progression in the internet game. We&#8217;ve seen CSS 2, CSS 3. We&#8217;ve seen HTML get a little bit better here and there, standards have improved. But nothing like AI. I mean, I remember the first time I typed a question into a bot and got a response back and I was like, okay, this is going to change things.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So yeah, we&#8217;re definitely in a big evolution here and one that I think is way more rapid than internet hitting us as well.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:05:58] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> I think the bit that makes it so profound is the fact that we&#8217;ve given over so much of ourselves to the internet. So during the .com boom, there were, I don&#8217;t know, how many desktop computers in the UK. I imagine there&#8217;s significantly more than there were then than there are now. You know, you had to go and find a computer, sit at a computer, switch the computer on, use things like dial up modems to get access.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:06:22] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> Yeah.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:06:23] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> And all of that kind of stuff, many obstacles. And now everything is in your pocket. So we do commerce in our pocket. We communicate from our, the device in our pocket. In other words, everything is wedded to the internet.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So the idea of things changing rapidly because we&#8217;ve become so accustomed to the internet being kind of like a utility. The rapid change is more profound, even if the change itself might not be so rapid.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Can I actually go back to the .com boom and bust bit? Because I bet a significant proportion of the people listening to this won&#8217;t even know what that was. Do you want to describe it, and how it crept up on you and overnight just decimated probably billions of dollars from stock markets?</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:07:05] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> Yeah, it was around about, I want to say 2000, right when it hit. And when we first started as an agency, I was building websites using university computers. I didn&#8217;t own a computer. This is what you were talking about. Not everybody could have afford that technology back then.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">But we were building websites for the most bizarre stuff. We had people coming in saying, I want to sell curry online. And I want to, anything and everything that they could come up with as with an idea, they were getting funding for.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">And we were dealing with small one man bands, but we&#8217;re also dealing with big companies that had huge investment, that was pumped into these internet ideas. And it&#8217;s kind of similar to what&#8217;s happening with AI right now.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">There&#8217;s an immense amount of money being invested into it. And the internet went from a handful of university websites, to everybody doing all manner of things online. The investment just got over the top. And then at some point somebody went, you know what, these investments have no ROI on them. There&#8217;s huge amounts of money being poured into this. And, basically the stock market crashed as a result of it.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">And we had companies literally within a week saying, we are no longer going to be continuing our management contracts with you. Or we don&#8217;t want to build this website with you anymore. We were building $2 &#8211; 300,000 websites for the likes of Safeway and Sony and KPMG and all kinds of stuff. And it just stopped overnight. And it caused a lot of agencies to fall over, just completely fail, within a matter of months.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:08:37] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> I remember seeing an ad, I was on Kensington High Street in London. I used to work on Kensington High Street, and I remember seeing an ad on a bus stop. So it was a prime bit of real estate in the UK, probably one of the more expensive places that you could put an ad. And it was for an internet product. And the product was that they would come to your workplace, take your shirt, iron it, and bring it back to you. And I remember looking at ad and thinking, is that really what they&#8217;re selling? Genuinely? Like, is there a market for that? And obviously, within a month the .com bubble had burst.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">But it was on that level, wasn&#8217;t it? It was the most peculiar ideas that people could come up with. And maybe some of them would&#8217;ve swum now. But back then, everything was sort of sinking. But also nobody had a mechanism to pay for it. We didn&#8217;t have the sort of access to credit and credit cards and Stripe and all of those payment gateways and all of that.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">It was just, we were falling over ourselves to put ideas out there. Nobody seemed to have the capacity to say, no, look objectively, that&#8217;s a silly idea. That there&#8217;s no business there. It was more, let&#8217;s try it.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">And then eventually a bit like happened with the Dutch and the tulips way back. Somebody woke up and said, hang on, there&#8217;s no money being generated by these things. Then presumably the bankers all took one look at each other and went, oh, what have we done.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:10:04] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> Yeah. And there were a lot of big, big ideas out there that caused a lot of that. I had one guy that came in and I won&#8217;t mention the project name, to save the guy&#8217;s grace. But he had come up with an idea to do an online training platform for children. And this guy we found out afterwards had maxed out his credit cards, had remortgaged his house, had got divorced from his wife, and a tiny part of the reason for the collapse around 2000. But it was that kind of thing that was happening all over the place.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:10:35] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> Yeah.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:10:36] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> We are seeing a similar thing happening now with AI. There&#8217;s a huge amount of investment going into it. Now I&#8217;m in no way an expert in the financial world and of investment in AI platforms and stuff, but hopefully we don&#8217;t see as big a bang as we did back then.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:10:50] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> It felt, at the time, it felt really normal until it suddenly didn&#8217;t. Until I saw that ad for that shirt ironing business, I genuinely I just thought, oh, that&#8217;s a good idea. Oh, that&#8217;s interesting. Look what they&#8217;re doing. Look what you can do now. Ads over and over again.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">But then as soon as it starts to unravel, you begin to get the intuition. Wow, that really was hysteria. We were all caught up in this hysteria. Whether or not you are a financial expert, it must be obvious to everybody that we are caught up in some kind of AI hysteria at the moment.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Now, it may be that 80% of all of the business ideas based around AI will succeed. Maybe it&#8217;ll be that 80% of them will fail. But have you got any intuitions that point to it being over egged, hysteria? Are there any things that you&#8217;ve seen out in the real world where you&#8217;ve thought, boy, that really does feel like a moment where we ought to be thinking about whether we&#8217;re caught up in mass hysteria here?</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:11:48] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> I think AI for me is like the next logical progression. We&#8217;re talking about coding here, right? AI is used for lots of different things, but in terms of coding, it&#8217;s like the next logical transition in terms of making coding accessible.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So if I think back to when I was in college and I was writing machine code for chips, literally like bite by bite, that was quite complicated. And then came along programming languages where we could not have to worry about the machine coding. And then we&#8217;ve got things like, for example Basic on a computer, which meant you didn&#8217;t have to worry about some of the more underlying code underneath that.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">And then came the internet with HTML. And then we had page builders. Now I&#8217;ve got AI where we don&#8217;t even have to think about writing a line of code. You can literally just ask it what you want, and hopefully you&#8217;ll get out of it what you want. At every stage it has made working with a computer, and making a computer do something more accessible to more people.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So now my father can just go online and say, hey, build me this website. I want a green background. I want this font, I want it to say this. Write me a paragraph about such and such, and they can pretty much whip something up in an afternoon. A year ago, two years ago, that was him having to learn how to code to do that, or would have to use a drag and drop page editor to do that. So it really has opened up the ability to develop software and websites to a lot more people.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The thing that I&#8217;m seeing is there&#8217;s an awful lot of ideas being developed. People are coming up with ideas. Some people not actually thinking about, is this a project that&#8217;s got legs? Do people need this? There&#8217;s a lot of repetition going on. There&#8217;s a lot of things being built where really, even if you build a website solution or a piece of software, you&#8217;ve still got to market that. You&#8217;ve still got to support it. You&#8217;ve still got to get it out to the masses.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">And AI&#8217;s not quite there with that yet. I can&#8217;t just say, right I&#8217;ve built this product, go and market it for me. It&#8217;s still going to need some feet on the ground to actually go out there and sell a product.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">When I got into software and I developed WS Form, building the actual plugin was a lot easier than actually getting out there and getting people interested in it, and telling people about why it&#8217;s a good product. That&#8217;s what took the time.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:13:57] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> That&#8217;s a curious insight. You said multiple things.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So there&#8217;s this person that I follow online, I won&#8217;t mention their name. It was really good little app and it served its purpose. It was quite a small thing.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Now, a year later, I would think it&#8217;s probably fair to say they&#8217;ve probably got about 15 apps. Is this just a case of just, okay, let&#8217;s build another and another and just keep going through that process. Selling, selling more, selling, more selling. But at what point do the people buying that app get the support that they need and the bug fixes that they need? And that I think is maybe a moment that we haven&#8217;t yet faced.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:15:02] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> Yeah.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:15:02] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> The marketing bit. You&#8217;ve built a plugin with AI for WordPress, but you don&#8217;t have the capacity to market it. So you build another one, and you build another one, and you build another one. But the few people that bought it, they&#8217;re going to expect updates and what have you. What I&#8217;m basically trying to do is agree with what you said. The wider bit is the harder bit. The selling it, not just building it, and then making sure that those people come back for more later.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:15:25] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> Yeah, I think that&#8217;s one part that a lot of people are missing. I&#8217;m seeing a lot of people saying, hey, I built this and I&#8217;m making X million per month. I don&#8217;t believe a lot of those posts that you see, with these get rich. If that was the case, everyone would be doing it.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">But, I love AI. And there are so many people online, on social media that are trying to tell you the best way of doing it. Everyone&#8217;s got their own, way of doing it. I&#8217;ve had to switch a lot of it off. It&#8217;s overwhelming.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:16:29] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> Okay, so that&#8217;s a curious insight. And it was where I was going to go next. In order for us to to buy all these things, we must all be earning to pay for all these things. And in order to earn, we all need to be selling our own things or doing some kind of lucrative work somewhere. You know to shell out for these things.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">That&#8217;s the bit that I&#8217;m concerned about. So that&#8217;s already competitive.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Now if you flood the zone with 10 more, 15 more, a hundred more, that means it&#8217;s much more difficult for everybody to achieve the sort of the weight that they need to have to make that profitable.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">It&#8217;s very hard to encapsulate what I&#8217;m trying to say, but it basically boils down to what is our capacity to attend to all of this stuff. And I was thinking this morning, coincidentally before you and I joined the call, I was thinking before computers came along, I know life wasn&#8217;t as rich and wasn&#8217;t as varied, but it was a heck of a lot less noisy. And we really do allow a lot of noise to get into our lives. And it feels like AI, WordPress, it&#8217;s very noisy at the moment. And I don&#8217;t think we&#8217;ve yet crossed that Rubicon. The noise, it&#8217;s become obvious to us how disruptive that noise was.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:18:07] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> Yeah.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:18:08] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> I think we&#8217;re still, in many cases, plugin vendors and theme vendors and things are still relying on previous year&#8217;s subscription to renew, but it&#8217;s the next year that I worry about and the year after that. And I don&#8217;t know if you&#8217;ve got any wisdom there.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:18:23] <strong>Mark Westguard:</strong> It&#8217;s an interesting time. I think there&#8217;s a lot of experimenting going on that is being portrayed as the way to do things. There&#8217;s a lot of people, I guess there&#8217;s a lot of people panicking. There&#8217;s even a lot of people in the WordPress world that have gone from WordPress to now saying, I&#8217;m not doing WordPress anymore, and they&#8217;re now a hundred percent AI. And, are they actually producing things that are making them a living? Other than maybe, some of these online content producers. Are there solid products that are coming out of AI?</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you just look at the plugin repo, the number of plugin submissions has gone through the roof because so many people can now just produce utility plugins so quickly and easily. There are still only a handful of people out there actually producing proper products, that are selling as plugins for WordPress, sizable plugins that are really expensive to build with AI. I&#8217;m fortunate that I didn&#8217;t have a suite of small utility plugins. I had quite a large form engine that I intentionally built to be quite complicated, and that&#8217;s kept me afloat.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:19:23] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> Oh, I see. You&#8217;ve got like a walled garden around your product a bit because of its complexity, but also it&#8217;s not a utility in the sense that you toggle something on and then something happens on your website. I don&#8217;t know, SMTP plugin or something like that. Bye.</p>\n</div></details>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">On the podcast today we have <a href=\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/westguard/\">Mark Westguard</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Mark has been building websites for over 30 years, starting out in the early days of the web with his agency Absolute Internet in the UK, before moving to the US and transitioning into product development. He now runs WS Form, a form plugin for WordPress.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">During this episode we get into the evolution of the internet and WordPress through major technological inflection points, most recently, the rise of AI. Mark draws comparisons between the AI era and the frenzy of the dot-com boom, sharing how rapid influxes of investment and hype can reshape the entire landscape, often faster than the underlying technology can deliver sustainable results.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The conversation focused not just on the opportunities AI brings to developers and end-users, such as dramatically lowering the barrier to entry for building websites or plugins, but also the risks and challenges. We chat about the overwhelming pace of new product creation, and how the real difficulty today isn’t necessarily building a plugin, but successfully marketing, supporting, and sustaining it in a crowded ecosystem.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Towards the end we talk about the potential saturation of plugin marketplaces and what this means for the future of WordPress businesses, especially as AI continues to accelerate product development. Mark says that he&#8217;s embracing AI in his own workflow, but has some nostalgia for the days when coding was a hands-on craft.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Several other points were raised, including the ongoing demand for trusted, supported software among non-developers, the looming spectre of AI-driven security vulnerabilities, and whether traditional CMS platforms like WordPress might see a renaissance if AI hype collapses.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you’re curious about how AI is shaping, and challenging, the future of WordPress development and product businesses, this episode is for you.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Useful links</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https://wsform.com\">WS class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The debates and fire around authorship are playing out faster in literature and other languages than you may be aware of. The best summation of this so far is <a href=\"https://thepointmag.com/examined-life/the-part-about-the-machines/\">Brunella Tipismana Urbano&#8217;s translated from Spanish essay The Part About the Machines</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<blockquote class=\"wp-block-quote is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The role of intellectuals is to shed light on these crossroads, to, as George Scialabba <a href=\"https://www.jstor.org/stable/25007119?seq=1\">once wrote</a>, understand their contemporary moral and political significance for the present with singular originality and force. Please do not install, run, or test this version of WordPress on production or mission-critical websites. Instead, it’s recommended to evaluate RC1 on a test server and site.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 RC1 can be tested using any of the following methods:</p>\n\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-table\"><table class=\"has-fixed-layout\"><tbody><tr><td><strong>Plugin</strong></td><td>Install and activate the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-beta-tester/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WordPress Beta Tester</a> plugin on a WordPress install. (Select the “Bleeding edge” channel and “Beta/RC Only” stream.)</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Direct Download</strong></td><td>Download the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/wordpress-7.2-RC1.zip\">RC1 version (zip)</a> and install it on a WordPress website.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Command Line</strong></td><td>Use this <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/cli/\">WP-CLI</a> command: <br /><code>wp core update --version=7.2-RC1</code></td></tr><tr><td><strong>WordPress Playground</strong></td><td>Use the <a href=\"http://playground.wordpress.net/release\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">WordPress Playground instance</a> to test the software directly in your browser.  No setup required – just click and go!</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The scheduled final release date for WordPress 7.2 is <strong>December 8, 2026</strong>. The full <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/2026/10/06/wordpress-7-2-release-party-schedule/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">release schedule can be found here</a>. Your help testing Beta and RC versions is vital to making this release as stable and powerful as possible. Thank you to everyone who contributes by testing!</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Please continue checking the <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Make WordPress Core blog</a> for <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/tag/7-2\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">7.2-related posts</a> in the coming weeks for more information.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Core Changes Since Beta 5</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Check out the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/news/2026/10/wordpress-7-2-beta-1-is-ready-for-download-and-testing/\">Beta 1 announcement</a> for details on WordPress 7.2.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can browse the technical details for all issues addressed since Beta 5 using these links:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/query?changetime=11%2F10%2F2026..11%2F17%2F2026&amp;milestone=7.2&amp;status=closed&amp;group=component&amp;order=id&amp;col=id&amp;col=milestone&amp;col=owner&amp;col=type&amp;col=priority\">Closed 7.2 WordPress Core Trac tickets</a> since November 10, 2026</li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/commits/wp/7.2?since=2026-11-10&amp;until=2026-11-17\">7.2 Gutenberg commits</a> since November 10, 2026</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 Release Candidate 1 brings additional bug fixes, accessibility improvements, and compatibility updates as the release moves closer to its December 8 launch.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Recent refinements include fixes to client-side media uploads, improvements to the new random-content redirect feature, and better keyboard navigation through a skip link in the WordPress admin bar. Additional updates improve plugin dependency messaging, script module translations, and the reliability of the HTML and Interactivity APIs.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Developers will also benefit from continued Abilities API refinements and updates to bundled libraries, including PHPMailer, Requests, getID3, and sodium_compat.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66490\">Trac #66490</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66681\">Trac #66681</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66625\">Trac #66625</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66331\">Trac #66331</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/66591\">Trac #66591</a>, <a href=\"https://core.trac.wordpress.org/ticket/65984\">Trac #65984</a></p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Additional Block Editor Improvements</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress 7.2 RC1 includes further refinements to the block editor, addressing issues with duplicated block content, keyboard interactions on iOS, and revision previews. Improvements to the Interactivity API prevent outdated navigation responses from replacing newer content, while additional fixes improve plugin installation workflows, featured image compatibility, and link suggestions. The transition to modern SVG icons also continues throughout WordPress administration.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Learn more:</strong> <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/86839\">GitHub #86839</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/86703\">GitHub #86703</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/86608\">GitHub #86608</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/78367\">GitHub #78367</a>, <a href=\"https://github.com/WordPress/gutenberg/pull/86854\">GitHub #86854</a></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Want to know more about this release? Here are some highlights:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>New Ipsum default theme</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Expanded responsive design and Gallery controls</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Improved Global Styles and typography tools</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Enhanced Canvas Notes and collaboration</li>\n\n\n\n<li>New DataForm block settings inspector</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Faster media uploads and image processing</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Modernized WordPress admin with SVG icons</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Faster admin navigation and asset loading</li>\n\n\n\n<li>New “On This Day” widget and random post discovery</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Improved translation update management</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Accessibility, security, and PHP 8.6 compatibility improvements</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How you can contribute</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress is open source software made possible by a passionate community of people collaborating on and contributing to its development. The resources below outline various ways you can help the world’s most popular open source web platform, regardless of your technical expertise.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Get involved in testing</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Testing for issues is crucial to the development of any software. It’s also a meaningful way for anyone to contribute. </p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Your help testing the WordPress 7.2 RC1 version is key to ensuring that the final release is the best it can be. While testing the upgrade process is essential, trying out new features is equally important. This <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/2026/10/20/help-test-wordpress-7-2/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">detailed guide</a> will walk you through testing features in WordPress 7.2.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Update your theme or plugin</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">For plugin and theme authors, your products play an integral role in extending the functionality and value of WordPress for all users.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Thanks for continuing to test your themes and plugins with the WordPress 7.2 beta releases. With RC1, you’ll want to conclude your testing and update the <em>“Tested up to”</em> version in your <a href=\"https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/how-your-readme-txt-works/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">plugin’s readme file</a> to 7.2.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you find compatibility issues, please post detailed information to the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/support/forum/alphabeta/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">support forum</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Test on your hosting platforms</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Web hosts provide vital infrastructure for supporting WordPress and its users. Testing on hosting systems helps inform the development process while ensuring that WordPress and hosting platforms are fully compatible, free of errors, optimized for the best possible user experience, and that updates roll out to customer sites without issue.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Want to test WordPress on your hosting system? <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/hosting/test-results-getting-started/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Get started with configuring distributed hosting tests here</a>. Thank you to all <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/hosting/test-results/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">web hosts who help test WordPress</a>!</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Curious about testing releases in general? Follow along with the <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/test/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">testing initiatives in Make Core</a> and join the <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/messages/core-test/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">#core-test channel</a> on <a href=\"https://wordpress.slack.com/\" rel=\"noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Making WordPress Slack</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">An RC1 haiku</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>The final bugs fall</em><br /><em>Release candidate takes flight</em><br In this video I walk through Create Not Learn, an experimental plugin that explores one possible answer. It uses everyday words, starts from tasks rather than menus, and keeps the complex tools out of the way until you need them. We’re using it to test ideas with real people…</p>\n<p><a href=\"https://aheadcreative.co.uk/articles/what-if-wordpress-was-built-for-users-not-developers-create-not-learn/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Source</a></p>\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:7:\"pubDate\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:31:\"Fri, 13 Nov 2026 14:55:52 +0000\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:10:\"Dave Smith\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}}}i:17;a:6:{s:4:\"data\";s:21:\"\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";s:5:\"child\";a:2:{s:0:\"\";a:5:{s:5:\"title\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:68:\"Open Channels FM: AI and the Changing Landscape of Game Preservation\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"guid\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:34:\"https://openchannels.fm/?p=2564536\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"link\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:75:\"https://openchannels.fm/ai-and-the-changing-landscape-of-game-preservation/\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:11:\"description\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:384:\"Artificial intelligence is reshaping the way classic video games are preserved and enjoyed. [&#8230;]\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:7:\"pubDate\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:31:\"Fri, 13 Nov 2026 14:06:35 +0000\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:16:\"Open Channels FM\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}}}i:18;a:6:{s:4:\"data\";s:21:\"\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";s:5:\"child\";a:2:{s:0:\"\";a:5:{s:5:\"title\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:57:\"WordPress.org blog: Copy Your WordPress Site with Reprint\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"guid\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:35:\"https://wordpress.org/news/?p=22001\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"link\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:53:\"https://wordpress.org/news/2026/11/reprint-your-site/\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:11:\"description\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:4378:\"<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Reprint is a free, open-source, PHP-based tool that copies a WordPress site’s files and database. The plugin is available in the <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/plugins/reprint/\">WordPress.org Plugin Directory</a>, along with the command-line client used to copy the site.</p>\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-post-featured-image\"><img alt=\"\" class=\"attachment-post-thumbnail size-post-thumbnail wp-post-image\" height=\"1440\" src=\"https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/11/reprint-hero-scaled.png?resize=2560%2C1440&#038;ssl=1\" width=\"2560\" /></figure>\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress has long let you <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/documentation/article/tools-export-screen/\">export your posts and pages</a>. Moving the site itself means bringing along its theme, plugins, uploads, and settings, too. Making that easier has been part of <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/2024/06/21/data-liberation-and-wordpress-migrations/\">WordPress’s Data Liberation work</a> since 2024.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Example use cases</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can use Reprint to copy your site to a new host, or to create a local copy meant for testing.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">While moving your site to a new host, visitors can keep using the original. Use Reprint to copy the site, and then open the copy to check that your pages load, your images appear, and your forms work before you send visitors there. When you’re ready to switch, pause changes on the original site, make one last copy, and point your domain to the new host. The <a href=\"https://developer.wordpress.org/reprint/guides/move-to-another-host/\">step-by-step guide to moving your site</a> walks you through the move.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You can also use Reprint to create a site copy meant for testing, a place to try changes without affecting the site your visitors use. <a href=\"https://developer.wordpress.org/reprint/getting-started/copy-a-site/\">Copy your site to your computer</a> or try the <a href=\"https://playground.wordpress.net/?clone-site=https%3A%2F%2Freprint-demo.wordpress.net\">experimental, browser-based option in WordPress Playground</a>. Use the copy to try a new layout with your existing pages, see how a plugin fits into your site, or check that everything still works after a WordPress update.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">How Reprint copies a site</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Reprint sends your files and database directly to the computer or server you choose, one piece at a time, without needing to connect to an external service. The transfer can begin without your old host having to make room for an archive of the whole site.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If the connection drops during the copy, you can resume from the last saved point. If you give Reprint a new site address, it can update links that point to the old one. It builds on the <a href=\"https://make.wordpress.org/core/2025/11/27/wordpress-importer-can-now-migrate-urls-in-your-content/\">WordPress Importer’s approach to rewriting links</a> and applies it to the copied database, including links stored in site settings, blocks, shortcodes, and complex data structures like serialized data or custom fields safely without corrupting tables.</p>\n\n\n\n		<figure class=\"wp-block-jetpack-videopress jetpack-videopress-player\">\n			<div class=\"jetpack-videopress-player__wrapper\"> </div>\n			\n			\n		</figure>\n		\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">When copying a site repeatedly, Reprint figures out what changed since the last time and copies only the files it needs.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Try Reprint</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">You don’t need to connect your own site to see how Reprint works. Try the <a href=\"https://playground.wordpress.net/?clone-site=https%3A%2F%2Freprint-demo.wordpress.net\">experimental, browser-based option in WordPress Playground</a>. You can copy a sample site right in your browser, with nothing to install.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">When you’re ready to work with your own pages and settings, start with a test site you manage. The <a href=\"https://developer.wordpress.org/reprint/getting-started/copy-a-site/\">guide to making your first copy</a> takes you from installing the plugin to opening the copied site on your computer.</p>\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:7:\"pubDate\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:31:\"Fri, 13 Nov 2026 12:00:00 +0000\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:15:\"Adam +0000\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}s:32:\"http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/\";a:1:{s:7:\"creator\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:16:\"Open FM\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}}}}i:20;a:6:{s:4:\"data\";s:21:\"\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n	\n\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";s:5:\"child\";a:2:{s:0:\"\";a:5:{s:5:\"title\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:86:\"WordCamp Feni\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"guid\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:40:\"https://central.wordcamp.org/?p=16786906\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"link\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:97:\"https://central.wordcamp.org/news/2026/11/wordpress-campus-connect-compact-polytechnic-institute/\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:11:\"description\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:16569:\"<div class=\"wp-block-group\"><div class=\"wp-block-group__inner-container is-layout-constrained wp-block-group-is-layout-constrained\">\n<div class=\"wp-block-group is-vertical is-layout-flex wp-container-core-group-is-layout-4fc3f8e1 wp-block-group-is-layout-flex\">\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading has-black-color has-text-color has-link-color wp-elements-2\">Campus Connect event</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">On November 12, 2026, WordPress Campus Connect took place at Compact Polytechnic Institute, Feni, Bangladesh, bringing together students, WordPress enthusiasts, speakers, and community members for a day of learning, knowledge sharing, and exploring opportunities within the global WordPress ecosystem.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The event was designed to introduce students to WordPress, modern website development, open-source contributions, plugin development, artificial intelligence, and career opportunities in the WordPress ecosystem.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Throughout the event, participants had the opportunity to learn from experienced speakers, explore different areas of WordPress, and discover how they can become active members of the global WordPress community.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Event Sessions and Speakers</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The event featured five sessions covering different aspects of WordPress, from modern website development to community contributions and career opportunities.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">1. Building Modern Websites with Blocks &amp; Full Site Editing</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Speaker:</strong> <a href=\"https://events.wordpress.org/campusconnect/2026/compact-polytechnic-institute-feni/speaker/muhibul-haque/\">Muhibul Haque</a></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Time:</strong> 10:15 AM (+06)</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This session focused on the modern WordPress editing experience and how the Block Editor (Gutenberg) and Full Site Editing (FSE) are transforming website development.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Participants explored different types of WordPress blocks, learned how to combine blocks and patterns to create engaging page layouts, and discovered how block themes work.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The session also covered customizing essential website elements, including headers, footers, templates, and navigation, directly through the WordPress Site Editor.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Key topics included:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Introduction to the WordPress Block Editor (Gutenberg).</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Exploring different block types and their practical uses.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Creating page layouts using blocks and patterns.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Understanding Full Site Editing (FSE).</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Working with block themes.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Customizing headers, footers, templates, and navigation.</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">2. Building the WordPress Ecosystem Through Plugin Development</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Speaker:</strong> <a href=\"https://events.wordpress.org/campusconnect/2026/compact-polytechnic-institute-feni/speaker/foysal-imran/\">Foysal Imran</a></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Time:</strong> 11:00 AM (+06)</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress is not just a website-building platform; it is a growing open-source ecosystem powered by developers and contributors worldwide.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This session introduced participants to the role of plugin development in extending WordPress functionality and solving real-world problems.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The discussion covered the fundamentals of WordPress plugin development, plugin architecture, and how developers can build custom solutions to meet different website requirements.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Participants also learned about contributing plugins to the official WordPress.org Plugin Directory and how open-source development can help them improve their technical skills, build portfolios, and explore career opportunities.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Key topics included:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Introduction to the WordPress ecosystem.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Understanding WordPress plugin architecture.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Fundamentals of plugin development.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Building solutions through custom plugins.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Contributing to the WordPress.org Plugin Directory.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Career opportunities through open-source development.</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">3. Getting Started with WordPress Contributions: Learn, Practice &amp; Contribute</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Speaker:</strong> <a href=\"https://events.wordpress.org/campusconnect/2026/compact-polytechnic-institute-feni/speaker/atikur-rahman/\">Atikur Rahman</a></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WordPress.org Profile:</strong> <a href=\"https://profiles.wordpress.org/atik0130961/\">Atikur Rahman (@atik0130961)</a></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Time:</strong> 11:30 AM (+06)</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I had the opportunity to lead this session and introduce students to the WordPress open-source community and the different ways they can contribute to the project.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">One of the most important messages I wanted to share was that contributing to WordPress is not limited to developers or people with advanced coding knowledge. Anyone interested in learning, sharing knowledge, and helping others can become part of this global community.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">We explored the different WordPress contribution teams and discussed how people with different skills and interests can contribute to the project.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I also introduced participants to WordPress.org and demonstrated some practical ways to get started with contributing.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The session covered three practical contribution opportunities:</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Creating a WordPress.org Account</strong></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Participants learned about creating their WordPress.org accounts, setting up their profiles, and becoming part of the WordPress community.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Contributing to the Polyglots Team</strong></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I introduced the WordPress Polyglots team, which works to make WordPress accessible to people around the world through translation.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">We explored how contributors can participate in translating WordPress, plugins, and themes into Bangla and help make WordPress more accessible to Bangla-speaking users.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Contributing to the Photo Directory</strong></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Participants also learned about the WordPress Photo Directory and how they can contribute photographs to this open-source resource.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This demonstrated that contributing to WordPress does not always require programming skills. People with different interests and abilities can make meaningful contributions.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Key topics included:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Introduction to the WordPress open-source community.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Understanding why WordPress contributions matter.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Exploring different WordPress contribution teams.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Ways to contribute beyond coding.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Getting started with WordPress.org.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Creating a WordPress.org account and setting up a profile.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Understanding the Polyglots team and Bangla translation.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Exploring the WordPress Photo Directory.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Practical demonstrations of contribution opportunities.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Building skills through open-source collaboration.</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">My goal was to help students understand that they do not need to wait until they become professional developers to start contributing. They can begin their contribution journey while learning.</p>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">4. WordPress &amp; AI: Exploring Smarter Tools and Practical Applications</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Speaker:</strong> <a href=\"https://events.wordpress.org/campusconnect/2026/compact-polytechnic-institute-feni/speaker/sayeeduzzaman-noor/\">Sayeeduzzaman Noor</a></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Time:</strong> 12:00 PM (+06)</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Artificial Intelligence is changing how websites are developed, content is created, and digital experiences are managed.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This session explored how AI tools can be integrated into everyday WordPress workflows to improve productivity, save time, and make website development more efficient.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Participants discovered how AI-powered tools and plugins can support website creation, content generation, SEO, coding, debugging, and design-related tasks.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The session also included practical examples of using AI in real-world WordPress projects.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Key topics included:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Introduction to AI in the WordPress ecosystem.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Exploring AI-powered WordPress tools and plugins.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Using AI for content creation.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Generating website copy and digital content.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>AI-assisted website development and debugging.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Improving productivity through AI tools.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Understanding the opportunities and limitations of AI.</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h3 class=\"wp-block-heading\">5. Opportunities for Students in WordPress</h3>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This session focused on the opportunities available to students who want to build a future in the WordPress ecosystem.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress offers opportunities beyond website development, including freelancing, entrepreneurship, open-source contributions, community participation, and professional collaboration.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Participants explored different career paths and learned why developing practical skills, building a portfolio, and participating in the community can help them grow professionally.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Key topics included:</p>\n\n\n\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Introduction to the WordPress ecosystem.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>WordPress career opportunities.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Freelancing and earning opportunities.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Open-source contributions.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Portfolio building and skill development.</li>\n\n\n\n<li>Learning roadmaps and career growth.</li>\n</ul>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">My WordPress Journey: From Student to Mentor to Community Speaker</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Being part of this event as a speaker was a meaningful experience for me.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">My journey with WordPress started as a student who wanted to learn how websites are built and understand the opportunities available in the WordPress ecosystem.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">As I continued learning, I discovered something even more valuable: WordPress is not only about building websites. It is also about learning together, sharing knowledge, helping others, and contributing to an open-source project used by people around the world.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">After becoming involved in the community, I started contributing to different areas of WordPress and supporting other learners in their journeys.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I completed the WordPress Credits course, became involved in community activities, and later took the opportunity to support students as a mentor.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">My involvement in the community gradually grew from being a learner to becoming a mentor and now having the opportunity to share my experience as a community speaker.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Standing in front of students at Compact Polytechnic Institute and sharing what I have learned throughout this journey was a special milestone for me.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I wanted students to understand that they do not need to know everything before getting involved. They can start with small steps, learn from the community, contribute according to their abilities, and gradually grow.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">A Learning Experience Beyond the Sessions</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Campus Connect is more than a series of presentations. It is an opportunity to introduce students to the possibilities of open-source collaboration and help them see how they can become part of a global technology community.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Through the different sessions, students were introduced to modern WordPress website development, plugin development, open-source contributions, AI-powered workflows, and professional opportunities.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Each session highlighted a different part of the WordPress ecosystem and showed how students can explore their interests, develop practical skills, and build their future.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I strongly believe that introducing students to open-source communities at an early stage can create valuable opportunities for both individual growth and the wider WordPress community.</p>\n\n\n\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">Thank You</h2>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">A special thanks to the organizers, speakers, teachers, and students of Compact Polytechnic Institute for making this Campus Connect event possible.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I am grateful for the opportunity to share my journey, discuss WordPress contributions, and encourage students to explore the global WordPress community.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I also appreciate the enthusiasm and participation of the students throughout the event.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I hope this event inspires more students to start learning WordPress, explore open-source contributions, and become active members of the community.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Looking forward to more opportunities to learn, contribute, mentor, and grow together.</p>\n</div>\n\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-gallery has-nested-images columns-default is-cropped wp-block-gallery-1 is-layout-flex wp-block-gallery-is-layout-flex\">\n<figure blog: WordCamp Asia 2027 Is Coming to Penang\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"guid\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:35:\"https://wordpress.org/news/?p=21919\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:4:\"link\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:55:\"https://wordpress.org/news/2026/11/wc-asia-2027-penang/\";s:7:\"attribs\";a:0:{}s:8:\"xml_base\";s:0:\"\";s:17:\"xml_base_explicit\";b:0;s:8:\"xml_lang\";s:0:\"\";}}s:11:\"description\";a:1:{i:0;a:5:{s:4:\"data\";s:29666:\"<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Next year, WordCamp Asia 2027 heads to Penang, Malaysia. From April 9 to 11, the WordPress community will gather at the Penang Waterfront Convention Centre for three days of contribution, learning, and connection. Tickets are on sale and sponsorship applications are open, so now is the time to make your plans.</p>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-82d461e8 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex\">\n<div class=\"wp-block-button\"><a class=\"wp-block-button__link has-background wp-element-button\" href=\"https://asia.wordcamp.org/2027/tickets/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Get Your Event Pass</a></div>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-button\"><a class=\"wp-block-button__link has-background wp-element-button\" href=\"https://asia.wordcamp.org/2027/call-for-sponsors/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\">Become a Sponsor</a></div>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-button\"><a class=\"wp-block-button__link has-background wp-element-button\" href=\"https://asia.wordcamp.org/2027/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\">About WordCamp Asia</a></div>\n</div>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">In 2026, <a href=\"https://wordpress.org/news/2026/04/celebrating-wcasia-2026/\">WordCamp Asia</a> brought 2,627 attendees to Mumbai, India. More than 1,500 participants joined Contributor Day, where table leads guided work on more than 20 contribution tables. The conference days that followed covered topics ranging from AI and developer workflows to enterprise WordPress and journalism on the open web, and closed with a panel featuring WordPress Executive Director <a href=\"https://profiles.wordpress.org/4thhubbard/\">Mary Hubbard</a>, <a href=\"https://profiles.wordpress.org/peterwilsoncc/\">Peter Wilson</a>, and <a href=\"https://profiles.wordpress.org/sergeybiryukov/\">Sergey Biryukov</a>.</p>\n\n\n\n<blockquote class=\"wp-block-quote is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WordPress is not a company. It is a shared commitment to keeping the web open.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Mary Hubbard, Executive Director, WordPress</em></p>\n</blockquote>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The mix of hands-on work and big-picture conversation is what sets a WordCamp apart. You learn from the people who build, scale, and maintain WordPress every day, trade ideas in the hallway between sessions, and head home with new collaborators and fresh energy for the work ahead.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Penang offers a memorable setting for WC Asia. George Town, a short drive from the venue, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, and National Geographic named Penang one of the best places in the world to travel to in 2027. The island is famous for its food, from internationally-recognized street food to fine dining, and it is one of Malaysia’s leading destinations for tourism. If you can, give yourself a day or two on either side of the conference to explore.</p>\n\n\n\n<figure class=\"wp-block-gallery alignfull has-nested-images columns-8 is-cropped class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">A regular ticket costs only USD $35, thanks to our sponsors. It covers both conference days, lunch and refreshments, and the after party. Tickets are released in batches, so buying early secures your place and helps organizers plan for everyone who is coming. Contributor Day on April 9 has its own registration, which opens January 27, 2027. It is limited to 600 participants.</p>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-cover wp-elements-2\"><img alt=\"\" class=\"wp-block-cover__image-background wp-image-21922 size-large\" height=\"683\" src=\"https://i0.wp.com/wordpress.org/news/files/2026/11/image.png?resize=1024%2C683&#038;ssl=1\" width=\"1024\" /><span class=\"wp-block-cover__background has-background-dim-70 has-background-dim\" style=\"background-color: #000073;\"></span><div class=\"wp-block-cover__inner-container is-layout-constrained wp-container-core-cover-is-layout-a6a02316 wp-block-cover-is-layout-constrained\">\n<p class=\"has-text-align-center has-white-color has-text-color has-link-color has-eb-garamond-font-family has-huge-font-size wp-elements-3 wp-block-paragraph\" style=\"margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0;\"><strong>Join Asia’s Largest WordPress Gathering</strong></p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"has-text-align-center wp-block-paragraph\">April 9 – 11, 2027 | Penang Waterfront Convention Centre (PWCC), Penang, Malaysia</p>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-3e41869c wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex\">\n<div class=\"wp-block-button\"><a class=\"wp-block-button__link has-vivid-red-background-color has-background has-eb-garamond-font-family has-large-font-size has-custom-font-size wp-element-button\" href=\"https://asia.wordcamp.org/2027/tickets/\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" target=\"_blank\"><strong>Get Your Event Pass</strong></a></div>\n</div>\n</div></div>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">None of this happens without sponsors. Their support keeps ticket prices low, backs the volunteers and contributors who make the event run, and helps WordCamp Asia stay true to the values of the WordPress project. In return, sponsors meet an engaged, international audience of developers, agencies, business owners, and creators.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Five packages are available, from a Small Business table to a Platinum booth, so there is a fit for local companies and global brands alike. Every sponsor also gets access to the Career Corner, a dedicated space for meeting and hiring WordPress professionals.</p>\n\n\n\n<div class=\"wp-block-group has-background is-layout-constrained My name is Nathan Wrigley.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Jukebox is a podcast which is dedicated to all things WordPress. The people, the events, the plugins, the blocks, the themes, and in this case, Drupal funding, community, and a shared journey with WordPress.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you&#8217;d like to subscribe to the podcast, you can do that by searching for WP Tavern in your podcast player of choice, or by going to wptavern.com/feed/podcast, and you can copy that URL into most podcast players.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you have a topic that you&#8217;d like us to feature on the podcast, I&#8217;m keen to hear from you and hopefully get you, or your idea, featured on the show. Head to wptavern.com/contact/jukebox, and use the form there.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So on the podcast today we have Tiffany Farriss. Tiffany has spent the last 30 years working in web design, development, and creation, and she&#8217;s been deeply embedded in the Drupal community for the past two decades. She&#8217;s currently the interim CEO of the Drupal Association and is the organization&#8217;s longest serving board member after Drupal&#8217;s founder Dries Buytaert.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">This episode is a bit of a departure as we usually focus squarely on WordPress, but Tiffany brings a wealth of experience from the broader PHP family, and this conversation explores the similarities and differences between the WordPress and Drupal ecosystems, particularly when it comes to infrastructure and the sustainability of open source projects.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Tiffany explains the maker-taker problem, and the challenges of sustaining free software platforms that end up in millions of sites across the web. She shares her insights on the real costs behind open source stewardship, drawing from both data and experience, and discusses how tiny groups have shouldered the burden for everyone, and why that might no longer be viable.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The discussion explores the Drupal Certified Partner Program, the push towards quantifying contributions, and how the open source community can improve transparency and sustainability.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Tiffany also touches on the role of public sector procurement in shaping the future of open source, the impact of new regulations, and how community driven recognition could incentivize more organisations to buy their round of beers for the ecosystem.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you&#8217;re interested in the sustainability of open source, the evolution of governance models, and how WordPress professionals can learn from the broader PHP landscape, this episode is for you.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">If you&#8217;re interested in finding out more, you can find all of the links in the show notes by heading to wptavern.com/podcast, where you&#8217;ll find all the other episodes as well.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">And so without further delay, I bring you Tiffany Farriss.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">I am joined on the podcast by Tiffany Farriss. Hello, Tiffany.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:03:19] <strong>Tiffany Farriss:</strong> Hi Nathan.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:03:20] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> Thank you for joining us. So this is a little bit of a departure. Usually we talk about WordPress. It&#8217;s WordPress on the menu every single day of the week. And that is going to be the case, I suspect a little bit today. But there&#8217;s also going to be a foray into Drupal because Tiffany is very much connected with the Drupal community.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So Tiffany, really just to get this podcast started, can I just offer you a chance to give us your little bio, tell us who you are and what you do over on the Drupal side, lo these many years. It&#8217;s been multiple decades that you&#8217;ve been involved in various different ways I think.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:03:52] <strong>Tiffany Farriss:</strong> It has been. Yeah. Thank you Nathan. I&#8217;m really glad to be here. I do consider all of us part of the PHP family, whether we&#8217;re Drupal or WordPress or Joomla or TYPO3, like I think we all have so much in common.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">But I am Tiffany Farriss. I&#8217;m currently the interim CEO over at the Drupal Association. I have been involved in web design, development, creation for the last 30 years. And I&#8217;ve been heavily involved in the Drupal community for the last 20.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">In particular in its governance. I&#8217;m the longest tenured board member of the Drupal Association, the 501 (c) (3) that is responsible for the governance. Other than Dries Buytaert, who is the founder of the Drupal Project.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">So I&#8217;ve been around a long time and I&#8217;m currently on leave from my own company, palantir.net to take a year to help Drupal kind of navigate this time of kind of intense disruption, I think it is for all of us. Pretty safe to say, that we are all experiencing the same disruptions from AI. And then just economic pressure in general.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">[00:04:50] <strong>Nathan Wrigley:</strong> So Drupal, I suppose, it would be a good thing to say at this point, Drupal is very similar in its project, in its status, in its goals, in its aims, in its infrastructure and technology to the CMS that most people listening to this are familiar with. It&#8217;s a PHP based CMS used by millions of websites all over the world. So broadly it&#8217;s the same.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Now you mentioned the foundation. In Drupal, we have the Drupal Association, which is an independent 501 (c) (3), which is a US-based not-for-profit. And we have specifically an educational and scientific mission that we&#8217;re authorised to do.</p>\n\n\n\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">We run Drupal.org. We run all of the infrastructure.
